Message from the Chairman                                       Introduction        from the President   and Three Strengths        Our Vision              Growth Strategy              Corporate Data




With pride in the growth on my watch,
I hand off to a new generation of management
with confidence in further growth                                                                                                                                           Kazuhiko Fujiwara
                                                                                                                                                                            Chairman and Representative Director
In the preceding fiscal year, we fulfilled the goals I pursued since I became President in
2018: net sales of ¥300 billion, and business profit of ¥30 billion, enabling us to deliver
returns to our shareholders and employees. That we were able to do so was thanks to
the dedication of every employee, to whom I extend my heartfelt gratitude. With our
Group’s management now generating real momentum, and to bring a more youthful
perspective to our leadership, I have decided that the timing is right to pass the baton to
my successor.
     During my time as President, I placed special emphasis on One Sumibe Activities.
By sweeping away the barriers between departments and encouraging lateral collabo-
ration, we built a structure that allows us to engage with customers as a single compa-
ny. I also visited research laboratories and plants across the country, engaging directly
with younger employees through numerous President’s talks, and constantly emphasiz-
ing the importance of taking on challenges. These accumulated efforts have gradually
transformed our internal culture, fostering a more vibrant atmosphere and promoting
innovative proposals from younger employees. Naturally, top-down decision-making is
essential in corporate management, but I am convinced that effective top-down leader-
ship must function hand in hand with a bottom-up culture that encourages the free flow
of opinions.
     Going forward, my job will be to fully support our new President, Shinichi Kajiya,
who has long been responsible for sales in our core businesses of semiconductor mate-
rials and high-performance plastics, and has a wealth of international experience.
Furthermore, he is well equipped with the human ability I have always valued. We have
always aimed to go beyond Customer Satisfaction (CS) to impress customers. To
achieve this, we must engage sincerely with customers and listen attentively to frontline
voices. From this standpoint I am convinced that President Kajiya is well equipped to
balance top-down and bottom-up and he moves forward, making him the leader we
need during the era that lies ahead. I therefore ask sincerely for the continued support
and cooperation of our stakeholders.

Message from the President   Introduction   from the President      and Three Strengths        Our Vision              Growth Strategy              Corporate Data




                                                     Building a corporate culture where
                                                     courageous risk-taking will be rewarded and
                                                     leveraging human resources and technology
Shinichi Kajiya                                      to achieve Vision 2030
President and
Representative Director

                                                            Opening the future through dialogue:
                                                            Thoughts on becoming President
                                                            I would like to express heartfelt gratitude to everyone involved for this opportunity to
                                                            embrace new challenges as President of Sumitomo Bakelite Co., Ltd. Facing the task of
                                                            leading such a historic enterprise, I also feel a profound sense of responsibility and deter-
                                                            mination. I am fully committed to guiding this wonderful organization, built by so many
                                                            predecessors, to even greater growth. Our Group is at the midway point of its Medi-
                                                            um-term Business Plan 2024–2026. Our Vision 2030 business profit target is ¥55 billion.
                                                            To achieve this, our vision of becoming “a company providing dreams for the future”
                                                            through value creation with our customers must be translated into concrete initiatives by
                                                            rapidly cycling through hypothesis and validation across the entire organization, and
                                                            converting those results into actionable initiatives. This effort will require close commu-
                                                            nication with customers and meticulous communication with employees. By listening
                                                            attentively to every voice and collaborating to lay out solutions to challenges, we will lay
                                                            a foundation for sustained growth, with eyes not only on 2030, but the horizon beyond.
                                                                 Our Group’s operating environment can be characterized as generally favorable. In
                                                            FY2024 we achieved ¥300 billion in sales revenue and ¥30 billion in business profit.
                                                            Underpinned by strong bonds of trust with customers, each business segment is grow-
                                                            ing steadily. We are pursuing top share in niche markets and earning strong global
                                                            recognition across three priority areas: ICT, including semiconductors and electronic
                                                            components; mobility, represented by high-performance plastics; and healthcare, where
                                                            we contribute to improving quality of life. At the same time, our external environment is
                                                            transforming rapidly under significant turbulence. We have entered an era where
                                                            conventional wisdom no longer applies, one shaped by the COVID-19 pandemic, conflict
                                                            in Ukraine, and the US tariff policies, to name a few. Amid such conditions, there are
                                                            limits to what we can achieve by going it alone. It is therefore essential to further
                                                            strengthen partnerships with external stakeholders, including customers, suppliers, and
                                                            research institutions, as well as to anticipate change and respond flexibly. Through
                                                            collaboration with external partners, we will aim to enhance our corporate value, becom-
                                                            ing a company where every employee can take pride in being part of our Group, and an
                                                            organization where individuals and the Company as a whole can grow.

developing minimally invasive medical devices and
expanding our product lineup, for example with gastro-
intestinal stents. Furthermore, in the pharmaceutical
packaging field, where we hold a strong domestic
market share, we are pursuing development of mono-
material PTP for international markets, with expecta-
tions for continued growth.
     Our Company is transforming its product portfolio,
and in the next phase, we will transform our business
portfolio. In addition to our three priority areas, we are
also considering approaches to strengthen life innova-
tion, which includes food packaging and construction-re-
lated business, and will conduct a Company-wide
review of our products and technologies. Through this
process, we will identify solutions that maximize
customer value, and consider measures such as M&A
and technology adoption strategically, to address
missing elements.
     SBinno, an internal program launched in 2016 as
one of our initiatives to create new products and
solutions, has begun to return results. A theme pursued
by employees selected to participate in SBinno’s first
year is moving toward commercialization as the
Brain-Machine Interface (BMI) Project Team, and we
expect similar initiatives to develop going forward.

History of Value Creation                                                     Introduction            from the President            and Three Strengths           Our Vision              Growth Strategy             Corporate Data


We have a history of plastics spanning more than 100 years and continues to evolve.
We will respond to social demands, expand business while creating new value, and strive for the realization of our aim to be “a company providing dreams for the future.”

Our Products That Support Society                               Introduction           from the President     and Three Strengths               Our Vision              Growth Strategy              Corporate Data


Our products, born from creative ingenuity, are utilized around the world and contribute to society in fields ranging from ICT, healthcare, and mobility to life innovation.
In ICT, our semiconductor materials are highly evaluated, and in the healthcare domain we provide products such as medical equipment and pharmaceutical packaging.
The mobility field covers a wide range of areas, including electric vehicles and aircraft,
while life innovation includes food packaging and construction materials.                                Semiconductor Materials    High-performance Plastics     Quality of Life Products

                Review of FY2024

Achieved business profit of ¥30.0 billion
and implemented measures to attain
                                                                                                       FY2024                    FY2025                       FY2026                        FY2030
our Vision 2030                                                                                         result                   forecast                      plan                          plan
In FY2024, profitability improved in semiconductor ma-
terials and quality of life products, with business profit
reaching ¥30.8 billion, exceeding the initial target of
¥30.0 billion. Semiconductor materials saw the Chinese                  Business
market remaining strong throughout the year, with                        profit               ¥30.8 billion                ¥32.5 billion                   ¥40.0 billion                ¥55.0 billion
sales of strategic products for mobility applications
growing steadily. In quality of life products, we have
seen substantial growth in the sale of films and sheets
for use in transporting electronic components and
                                                                  Business profit
semiconductor dicing processes. Other products
including medical equipment, industrial functional ma-            to revenue ratio                     10.1%                     10.4%                        11.5%                          13.0%
terials, and waterproofing-related products also per-
formed well overall. While high-performance plastics
performed well in the Asian market, the phenol-related
business in North America was sluggish, and an
impairment loss was recorded while promoting struc-
tural reforms. As a result, ROE was 6.5%.
                                                                  (Sales revenue)           (¥304.8 billion) (¥310.0 billion) (¥345.0 billion) (¥420.0 billion)
     To achieve our Medium-term Business Plan targets
and our Vision 2030, our Group will invest in develop-
ing new markets, new products that meet customer
needs, and a stable supply of high-quality products to
our customers. We have launched a project to com-                           ROE                        6.5%                       8.0%                         9.0%                          10.0%
mercialize Brain-Machine Interface (BMI) devices in
new markets, and are working to mass producing
them. In terms of production, we have established new
plants with cutting-edge automation systems in Nan-
tong and Suzhou, China, as well as Taiwan.
     In terms of shareholder returns, we have decided
                                                                   FY2024
                                                                   Annual dividend: ¥95.00/share Dividend payout ratio: 46% Share buyback: ¥20 billion
to pay an annual dividend of ¥95 per share, which
includes a commemorative dividend of ¥5 to mark our
70th anniversary (March 1, 2025). The dividend payout
ratio exceeded the 40% target set in the Medium-term               FY2025 (forecast)
                                                                   Annual dividend: ¥105.00 /share Dividend payout ratio: 39%
Business Plan to reach 46% (30% target in the previous
Medium-term Business Plan). Our policy is to continue
returning profits to shareholders on a stable, sustain-
able basis. Furthermore, we also repurchased treasury
shares worth ¥20.0 billion in February 2025.

Environmental Vision for 2050 (Net Zero)                                                    Environmental Vision

Our Group relies on using the Earth’s natural resourc-                                      01. We will realize carbon neutrality regarding GHG emissions (Scope 1 and 2) by 2050.
es, water, and energy in order to continue doing busi-                                         ●Reduce GHG emissions via energy conservation activities, MFCA*1 activities,

                                                                                                                                                               Executive Officer Structure/Executive Officers’ Meeting
                                                                                                                                                               We have introduced an Executive Officer structure in which Executive Officers
                                                                                                                                                               appointed by the Board of Directors as the persons responsible for business
                                                                                                                                                               execution execute business under the direction of the Company President based on
                                                                                                                                                               the policies determined by the Board of Directors, and have appointed 18 Executive
                                                                                                                                                               Officers (including five who serve concurrently as Directors).
                                                                                                                                                                    Executive Officers’ Meetings are held once a month and attended by Directors,
                                                                                                                                                               Executive Officers, and Corporate Auditors to convey information on policies and
                                                                                                                                                               important matters decided by the Board of Directors, to receive reports on business
                                                                                                                                                               performance and the status of business execution from each Executive Officer, and
                                                                                                                                                               to review important matters and share information.

                Executive Remuneration


Executive Remuneration

To provide an incentive to contribute to the sustained improvement of corporate value of our                                            corporate value and the standard composition ratio shall be set to be approximately 50%
Group for the medium- to long-term, the remuneration of Directors shall be based on a remu-                                             monthly remuneration, 40% bonus, and 10% stock compensation. However, the remuneration
neration system that is linked to management targets and shareholder value. Specifically,                                               of Outside Directors shall comprise exclusively their monthly remuneration in light of the fact
remunerations shall comprise “monthly remuneration,” which is a fixed remuneration accord-                                              that they supervise management from a position independent of business execution.
ing to position, “bonuses,” which are performance-linked and based on management indica-                                                     By resolution of the Board of Directors, the authority to decide on Director remuneration
tors set with a view to achieving the long-term visions, and “stock compensation,” which is a                                           for FY2024 has been delegated to the Nomination and Remuneration Committee.
form of non-monetary remuneration, as medium- to long-term incentives to encourage the                                                       The remuneration of Corporate Auditors consists of basic remuneration (monthly remu-
sharing of value with shareholders. The proportion of each type of remuneration shall be                                                neration). The total amount and the amount to be paid to each individual Corporate Auditor
appropriately determined by taking into account various factors such as position and respon-                                            are determined in consultation with the Corporate Auditors within the maximum remunera-
sibilities so that it functions as an incentive to contribute to the sustained improvement of our                                       tion limit resolved by the General Meeting of Shareholders.

Analyzing and Evaluating the Effectiveness of the Board of Directors

We evaluate the effectiveness of the Board of Directors each year. We enhance                                               the analysis results of the survey, summarizing the evaluation results based on
the effectiveness of the Board of Directors by executing the PDCA cycle of                                                  these discussions and setting the issues to be addressed, and implementing
conducting self-evaluations and aggregation of opinions based on a question-                                                initiatives to resolve these issues at the Board of Directors. The results of the
naire survey of all members of the Board of Directors (Directors and Corporate                                              analysis and evaluation of the effectiveness of the Board of Directors for FY2024

Ikoi no Mori biotope of the Shizuoka Plant. Ikoi
no Mori features lush forest and flowering
plants that change expression with each
season. It is visited by kingfishers and other
water birds. It is a place where quiet and motion
coexist, and healing scenery can be enjoyed.
